Process-group kill guard for spawned agent subprocesses.\n2\t//!\n3\t//! Depends on: nothing (leaf module — only `libc`).\n4\t\n5\tuse tokio::process::Child;\n6\t\n7\t// ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n8\t// ProcessGuard\n9\t// ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n10\t\n11\t/// Guard for a spawned child process that ensures cleanup on drop.\n12\t///\n13\t/// Wraps a `tokio::process::Child` and provides a `wait()` method. If dropped\n14\t/// before `wait()` is called, the child process is killed to prevent orphaning.\n15\tpub struct ProcessGuard {\n16\t    child: Option<Child>,\n17\t    pid: u32,\n18\t}\n19\t\n20\timpl ProcessGuard {\n21\t    /// Create a new ProcessGuard from a spawned Child.\n22\t    ///\n23\t    /// # Arguments\n24\t    /// * `child` - The spawned child process to guard\n25\t    pub fn new(child: Child) -> Self {\n26\t        let pid = child.id().unwrap_or(0);\n27\t        Self {\n28\t            child: Some(child),\n29\t            pid,\n30\t        }\n31\t    }\n32\t\n33\t    /// Get the process ID.\n34\t    pub fn id(&self) -> u32 {\n35\t        self.pid\n36\t    }\n37\t\n38\t    /// Wait for the child to exit and return its exit status.\n39\t    ///\n40\t    /// This consumes the guard and returns the exit status. After calling this,\n41\t    /// the child is considered reaped and no cleanup will be performed on drop.\n42\t    pub async fn wait(mut self) -> std::io::Result<std::process::ExitStatus> {\n43\t        if let Some(mut child) = self.child.take() {\n44\t            child.wait().await\n45\t        } else {\n46\t            Err(std::io::Error::other(\"child already reaped\"))\n47\t        }\n48\t    }\n49\t\n50\t    /// Get a mutable reference to the inner child (for operations like start_kill).\n51\t    pub fn get_mut(&mut self) -> Option<&mut Child> {\n52\t        self.child.as_mut()\n53\t    }\n54\t\n55\t    /// Create a future that waits for the child to exit without consuming the guard.\n56\t    ///\n57\t    /// This allows using the guard in select! branches where the guard needs to remain\n58\t    /// available for other operations. The future will wait for the child to exit\n59\t    /// and return the exit status, but the guard remains owned by the caller.\n60\t    ///\n61\t    /// After this future completes successfully, call `wait()` to consume the guard\n62\t    /// and mark it as reaped, or drop the guard to trigger cleanup.\n63\t    pub async fn wait_borrowed(&mut self) -> std::io::Result<std::process::ExitStatus> {\n64\t        if let Some(ref mut child) = self.child {\n65\t            child.wait().await\n66\t        } else {\n67\t            Err(std::io::Error::other(\"child already reaped\"))\n68\t        }\n69\t    }\n70\t}\n71\t\n72\timpl Drop for ProcessGuard {\n73\t    fn drop(&mut self) {\n74\t        if let Some(mut child) = self.child.take() {\n75\t            // Child was not waited on - kill it to prevent orphaning.\n76\t            // Best-effort: ignore errors if already dead.\n77\t            let _ = child.start_kill();\n78\t        }\n79\t    }\n80\t}\n81\t\n82\t// ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n83\t// ProcessGroupKillGuard\n84\t// ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n85\t\n86\t/// Kills a spawned process's group on drop unless [`disarm`](Self::disarm) was\n87\t/// called first.\n88\t///\n89\t/// A `tokio::time::timeout` wrapped directly around a spawn-and-wait future\n90\t/// (e.g. `Command::output()`) kills the child correctly when *that* timeout\n91\t/// fires: the future resolves to `Err(Elapsed)` and the caller's own match\n92\t/// arm can react. The gap is a timeout wrapping something *further up* the\n93\t/// call stack — Worker's mitosis-evaluation step wraps a whole multi-step\n94\t/// `evaluate()` call (prompt build, dispatch, response parse) in its own,\n95\t/// shorter, `tokio::time::timeout`; the weave strand does the same around its\n96\t/// whole `evaluate_internal()`. When that outer timeout fires first, it drops\n97\t/// the in-flight future — including whatever spawn-and-wait future was\n98\t/// nested inside it — before any inner match arm, kill logic, or cleanup code\n99\t/// gets a chance to run. Process-group kill guard for spawned agent subprocesses.\n//!\n//! Depends on: nothing (leaf module — only `libc`).\n\nuse tokio::process::Child;\n\n// ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// ProcessGuard\n// ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n/// Guard for a spawned child process that ensures cleanup on drop.\n///\n/// Wraps a `tokio::process::Child` and provides a `wait()` method. If dropped\n/// before `wait()` is called, the child process is killed to prevent orphaning.\npub struct ProcessGuard {\n    child: Option<Child>,\n    pid: u32,\n}\n\nimpl ProcessGuard {\n    /// Create a new ProcessGuard from a spawned Child.\n    ///\n    /// # Arguments\n    /// * `child` - The spawned child process to guard\n    pub fn new(child: Child) -> Self {\n        let pid = child.id().unwrap_or(0);\n        Self {\n            child: Some(child),\n            pid,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Get the process ID.\n    pub fn id(&self) -> u32 {\n        self.pid\n    }\n\n    /// Wait for the child to exit and return its exit status.\n    ///\n    /// This consumes the guard and returns the exit status. After calling this,\n    /// the child is considered reaped and no cleanup will be performed on drop.\n    pub async fn wait(mut self) -> std::io::Result<std::process::ExitStatus> {\n        if let Some(mut child) = self.child.take() {\n            child.wait().await\n        } else {\n            Err(std::io::Error::other(\"child already reaped\"))\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Get a mutable reference to the inner child (for operations like start_kill).\n    pub fn get_mut(&mut self) -> Option<&mut Child> {\n        self.child.as_mut()\n    }\n\n    /// Create a future that waits for the child to exit without consuming the guard.\n    ///\n    /// This allows using the guard in select! branches where the guard needs to remain\n    /// available for other operations. The future will wait for the child to exit\n    /// and return the exit status, but the guard remains owned by the caller.\n    ///\n    /// After this future completes successfully, call `wait()` to consume the guard\n    /// and mark it as reaped, or drop the guard to trigger cleanup.\n    pub async fn wait_borrowed(&mut self) -> std::io::Result<std::process::ExitStatus> {\n        if let Some(ref mut child) = self.child {\n            child.wait().await\n        } else {\n            Err(std::io::Error::other(\"child already reaped\"))\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Drop for ProcessGuard {\n    fn drop(&mut self) {\n        if let Some(mut child) = self.child.take() {\n            // Child was not waited on - kill it to prevent orphaning.\n            // Best-effort: ignore errors if already dead.\n            let _ = child.start_kill();\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// ProcessGroupKillGuard\n// ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n/// Kills a spawned process's group on drop unless [`disarm`](Self::disarm) was\n/// called first.\n///\n/// A `tokio::time::timeout` wrapped directly around a spawn-and-wait future\n/// (e.g. `Command::output()`) kills the child correctly when *that* timeout\n/// fires: the future resolves to `Err(Elapsed)` and the caller's own match\n/// arm can react. The gap is a timeout wrapping something *further up* the\n/// call stack — Worker's mitosis-evaluation step wraps a whole multi-step\n/// `evaluate()` call (prompt build, dispatch, response parse) in its own,\n/// shorter, `tokio::time::timeout`; the weave strand does the same around its\n/// whole `evaluate_internal()`. When that outer timeout fires first, it drops\n/// the in-flight future — including whatever spawn-and-wait future was\n/// nested inside it — before any inner match arm, kill logic, or cleanup code\n/// gets a chance to run. Dropping a future mid-`Child::wait()` does not kill\n/// the OS process; the child is simply orphaned, silently, indefinitely. This\n/// guard makes that outcome unreachable: however the future is torn down,\n/// `Drop` runs and reaps the process group. See bf-653n7.\npub struct ProcessGroupKillGuard {\n    pid: i32,\n    armed: bool,\n}\n\nimpl ProcessGroupKillGuard {\n    /// `pid` must be the direct child's PID *and* its process group ID —\n    /// i.e. the child must have been spawned with `setpgid(0, 0)` in a\n    /// `pre_exec` hook, so a group-wide kill here reaches subprocesses the\n    /// child itself forked (e.g. a shell exec'ing into a long-running CLI),\n    /// not just the direct child, without ever touching an unrelated group\n    /// (notably, the caller's own, if the child were left in the caller's\n    /// inherited group instead of its own).\n    pub fn new(pid: u32) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            pid: pid as i32,\n            armed: pid > 0,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Call once the process is confirmed reaped (normal exit, or after the\n    /// caller's own manual kill+wait) so `Drop` becomes a no-op instead of\n    /// signaling a process-group ID the kernel may since have reused.\n    pub fn disarm(&mut self) {\n        self.armed = false;\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Drop for ProcessGroupKillGuard {\n    fn drop(&mut self) {\n        if self.armed {\n            // SAFETY: killpg with SIGKILL on a plain pid_t is not memory-unsafe;\n            // libc::kill/killpg calls are marked unsafe only because they are\n            // FFI. Best-effort: ESRCH (already dead) is expected and ignored.\n            unsafe {\n                libc::killpg(self.pid as libc::pid_t, libc::SIGKILL);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n","numLines":143,"startLine":1,"totalLines":143}}}
